--- Page 66 ---
<meta>This page continues Jan Hus’s argument that the high status and legal primacy of the Pope are not divine in origin, but were granted by Roman Emperors. He argues that this worldly power actually hinders the spiritual mission of the Church.</meta>

# I. H. ON THE CHURCH

...obtained primacy over the four sees—Alexandria, Antioch, Jerusalem, and Constantinople—and stand as the most exalted leader of all priests in the entire world, and so forth. Behold, the Pope’s appointment and institution flowed from the power of Caesar <note>A reference to the "Donation of Constantine," a document (later proved to be a forgery) in which Emperor Constantine supposedly granted the Pope supreme authority over the Western Roman Empire.</note>, which cannot limit the power of God. Hence, later Popes, fearing they might lose that appointment, sought confirmation from other Caesars. Thus, in the 63rd Distinction, the Decree speaks as follows: "I, Louis, Roman Emperor Augustus, establish and grant by this pact of our confirmation to you, Saint Peter, prince of the Apostles, and through you to your Vicar, the Lord Pope Paschal, and to his successors forever, just as you have held and managed the city of Rome under our power and dominion from our predecessors until now." But we should not care for this grant of words when Caesar says, "I, Louis, grant to you, Saint Peter." Peter—then already an inhabitant of the kingdom of heaven—never needed civil Roman possession; nor was Louis ever greater than Peter, nor a truer possessor than Peter. Would that Peter, if it had been God’s will, had said: "I do not accept your grant; for while I was Bishop of Rome, I had left everything behind. I did not desire dominion over Rome from Nero, and I do not need it now. I see that it greatly harms my successors, for it hinders them in the preaching of the Gospel, in prayer for salvation, and in the fulfillment of God’s commands and counsels, and it makes many of them proud." Since, therefore, Almighty God can take away the privilege of these emperors and return His church to the state of equality among bishops as it was before the endowment <note>original: "dotationem"; the gifting of land and worldly wealth to the church.</note>, it follows that He can grant others—besides the Pope and cardinals—to be true successors in ministering to His church, just as the holy Apostles ministered.

<margin>Peter would not have accepted the dominion of modern Popes</margin>

But an objection is raised against this. For the Pope possesses such a constitution, but not as the Decree says in the 22nd Distinction, in the chapter *Sacrosancta* <note>A section of canon law concerning the "Sacrosanct" status of the Roman Church.</note>, where Pope Anacletus speaks thus: "The holy Roman church obtained its primacy not from the Apostles, but from the Lord Himself."

<margin>According to Anacletus, the Roman church obtained primacy from Christ.</margin>

<column-break/>

### CHAPTER XV
117
<meta>catchword: perhibe</meta>

...obtained. From which it follows that the Pope is instituted as so excellent not by an Emperor or a man, but immediately by God. And the same [secondly] is clear from the submission of kingdoms. And thirdly, from the testimony of the Doctors <note>The "Doctors" are the authoritative theologians and legal scholars of the Church.</note> speaking of the Pope’s power. To the first point, it must be assumed that this Pope understood the "holy Roman church" not as a stone or wooden Basilica, but as Saints Peter and Paul and the others who inhabited that place. Therefore, in the same Decree, he says that Peter and Paul were companions in the city of Rome; thus, when it is specifically said that it "obtained primacy," this context must be assumed. Secondly, he speaks of a primacy of men in the eyes of God, based on the first-fruits of virtues and the building up of the church, and not of a primacy of temporal riches or human glory—a primacy which Christ’s apostles rejected. And it is clear that the following argument is invalid: "The Roman church obtained primacy not from the Apostles, but from the Lord Himself; therefore, every Roman pontiff must excel in worldly glory or rule in a secular manner." Rather, it follows from the Decree that the Roman pontiff is bound to *serve* that people, not by lording it over them or suspending them <note>Refers to "suspension," a legal penalty in the church that bars someone from their office or from the sacraments.</note>, but by praying effectively for them, as the Decree alleges Paul did in Romans 1. He says: "Although the prayer of all the saints is poured out before the Lord for everyone, nevertheless, the most blessed Paul promises the Romans in his own handwriting, saying: 'For God is my witness, whom I serve in my spirit in the gospel of his Son, that without ceasing I make mention of you always in my prayers.'" From this Decree, it [does not] seem that Pope Anacletus wished to assert that he himself ought to rule civilly over all others, or have primacy in terms of dominion over all other persons of the Church Militant <term>Ecclesia Militans: the church on earth, currently struggling against sin and evil</term>; for by thus seeking his own glory (as it says in John 5), he would have the most evident sign of the Antichrist. Indeed, Boniface VIII, in his decree *Unam sanctam* <note>original: "extrauagatione Unam sanctam"; a 1302 papal bull asserting the Pope's absolute authority over both spiritual and temporal matters.</note>, did not dare to assert that expressly, because then only...

<margin>Anacletus explains.</margin>

<margin>The Pope should serve the faithful, not dominate them.</margin>

<summary>Jan Hus argues that papal primacy was historically granted by Roman Emperors like Constantine and Louis the Pious rather than by God. He asserts that true primacy should be based on spiritual virtue and service to the faithful, rather than worldly wealth and political dominion.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Jan Hus’s critique of the papacy's worldly power. Having argued that the Pope’s legal authority stems from Roman Emperors rather than divine mandate, he now addresses the implications of this "imperial" status and how it compares to the humility of the early Apostles.</meta>

# ON THE CHURCH
## CHAPTER XV

...should bear witness <note>original: "perhiberet testimoniū"</note> that he himself was a most holy man; but then both the faithful and the unfaithful could appropriately object to him, saying: "You bear witness of yourself; your witness is not true" <note>A reference to John 8:13. Hus argues that if the Pope claims his own holiness and authority, it is legally and spiritually suspect.</note>. From this, the Roman Pontiffs have become entangled in a perplexity arising from the endowment and exaltation of Caesar. When the Emperor asks them if they exceed all living mortals in power, primacy, and dignity regarding lordship, they feel they must concede it—for otherwise, as they say, no one would believe they are Popes.

However, Peter and Paul would not have conceded such a thing about themselves, because they did not have power given to them by Caesar. Therefore, Paul truly and humbly confessed in the first letter to the Corinthians, chapter 15: "I am," he said, "the least of the Apostles, who am not worthy to be called an Apostle" <note>1 Corinthians 15:9</note>. Why then would the Pontiff not profess the same, if imperial dignity did not stand in the way? Therefore, it is a true conclusion that the Pope holds the excellence of his dignity immediately from God, and not from a man who is not God, or from a mere human. But it is necessary that he deserve that dignity through humility, without pomp; and if Caesarean <note>imperial</note> dignity extols the Pope before the world, without humility and a holy life, it pertains to the life of Christ and His glory only insofar as he is an Antichrist worldly exalted.

<margin>To the second point</margin>
To the second point regarding the submission of kingdoms, it is said that this was initially involuntary, arising in a sense from fear of the Emperor's mandate. In this way, not all people ought to be subjected to the Pope as if he were a secular lord over them; therefore, that subjection does not prove a necessity for the Roman Pontiff to rule with Cardinals in such a way until the end of the world.

<margin>To the third point</margin>
To the third point, regarding the testimony of the Doctors <note>The "Doctors" refers to authoritative theologians and legal scholars of the Church.</note> speaking of the power of the Pope, it is said that all who so magnify the Pope's power—claiming that he can do whatever he wishes without blame, and that no one has the right to ask why he does so—are "Rulers of Lies," seducing the people of the Lord Christ. Such men should not be believed unless they have founded their words in Scripture <column-break/> pture.

For thus the great Doctor Augustine often asserts: that he should not be believed except insofar as he has grounded himself in Scripture. And it is clear that God can provide other successors to the Apostles besides the Pope and the Cardinals, just as He was able to provide others when the high priests of the Old Law, the scribes and the Pharisees with their traditions, did not keep the law of the Lord. To them He said in Matthew 21: "I say to you, the kingdom of God shall be taken from you, and given to a nation bringing forth its fruits" <note>Matthew 21:43</note>. The Savior said this to the priests when they brought judgment against themselves, saying: "He will miserably destroy those wicked men, and will let out his vineyard unto other husbandmen, which shall render him the fruits in their seasons" <note>Matthew 21:41</note>. How then is the hand of the Lord shortened, that He could not cast out the Pope and the Cardinals and appoint others who, without those titles, would build up the Church, as He did with the Apostles?

<margin>The Pope and Cardinals can be removed from the Church, just as the Pharisees were from the Synagogue.</margin>

Likewise, all bishops of Christ’s Church who follow Christ in their conduct are the true Vicars of the Apostles; and these are not [necessarily] the Pope and Cardinals. Therefore, other true successors of the Apostles can be found or given besides the Pope and Cardinals. This consequence is well known... and the first part is evident in the 21st Distinction, in the section "In novo" <note>A reference to the *Decretum Gratiani*, the fundamental collection of canon law.</note>, where Pope Anacletus says that the other Apostles received honor and power in equal fellowship with Peter. And he later says: "And when they passed away, Bishops rose up in their place." Regarding this, the gloss <note>An interpretive commentary written in the margins of legal or biblical texts.</note> says: "The argument is that every Bishop is equal to an Apostle regarding the order of consecration."

The same is evident in the 23rd Question, 1st Chapter, "It speaks to Peter," where Cyprian, bishop and martyr, says that Christ granted equal power to all the Apostles after His resurrection. This is also stated by the Blessed Jerome in the 95th Distinction, in the chapter "Olim" <note>Jerome was a 4th-century scholar; this specific reference points to his view that "bishop" and "presbyter" were originally the same office.</note>, where he says: "Of old, the presbyter was the same as the bishop, and before, by the instinct of the devil, factions were made, and it was said among the people, 'I am of Paul, I am of Apollos.'"

<margin>Argument II.</margin>

->Likewise<-

<summary>Jan Hus argues that the Pope's authority is not uniquely supreme by divine right, but was inflated by imperial decrees. He asserts that any bishop who follows Christ's example is a true successor to the Apostles and that God can replace a corrupt papacy just as He replaced the Pharisees.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Jan Hus’s argument that the institutional hierarchy of the Pope and Cardinals is not the only way the Church can exist. Having previously argued that the Pope's worldly status is a human invention of the Roman Emperors, Hus now uses historical precedents—like the Council of Pisa—and the writings of Church Fathers to show that the "primitive" church operated successfully with only simple priests and deacons, and could do so again.</meta>

<page-num>120</page-num>
<header>I. H. ON THE CHURCH</header>

<margin>Argument III.</margin>
Likewise, all the Archbishops, Patriarchs, and bishops at the Council of Pisa <note>The Council of Pisa (1409) was an assembly called to end the Western Schism; it deposed both rival popes to elect a new one, illustrating that a council could hold authority over a pope.</note>, in recognizing, defining, and condemning Pope Gregory XII as a heretic, were and are now true successors of the apostles. Yet they are distinct from the Pope and Cardinals. Therefore, the sixth point <note>referring to an earlier proposition by his opponents</note> is false; the logic follows from what was stated above, and the Doctors [of the Church] did not dare to deny this first part.

<margin>Argument IV.</margin>
Likewise, it would be evangelical wisdom for all priests to be holy and regulated immediately by the one and only pontiff, the Lord Jesus Christ. For it was so in the time of the apostles, when the church grew, and this opinion harmonizes with the scriptures. Therefore, God could thus lead his church back to its original state by removing the leadership of the Pope and the cardinals; consequently, others besides these can be the Vicars of the Apostles.

<margin>Argument V.</margin>
Likewise, the limit of the power and office of a minister of the church must be marked so that he does not wander astray; but there is no limit except that which Christ instituted. Since Christ is omnipotent, omniscient, and supremely benevolent, it is clear that reason requires his church to be ordered in an unchangeable way. This is especially true since in the early church the harvest was more plentiful and the number of workers was more abundant, yet God did not ordain a vast variety of ministers. At that time, he ordained only deacons and priests; then, the priest and the bishop were the same, as Jerome says <note>St. Jerome (c. 347–420 AD) was an influential scholar who argued that in the New Testament, "elder" (presbyter) and "bishop" (episcopus) referred to the same office.</note>, and as is clear from the Apostle Paul’s text to Timothy and Titus. From these, Jerome draws a conclusion in his letter to the priest Evagrius (found in Distinction 93, chapter "Legimus").

<margin>One church in all nations.</margin>
Hence he says there: "For what does a bishop do, except for ordination, that a priest does not do? Nor should the church of the city of Rome be considered one thing and the church of the whole world another. Gaul, Britain, Africa, Persia, the East, India, and all barbarian nations adore one Christ and observe one rule of truth. If authority is sought, the world <note>original: "orbis" (the world/the globe)</note> is greater than the City <note>original: "urbe" (specifically referring to Rome)</note>. Wherever there was a Bishop—whether at Rome, Constantinople, or Alexandria—he is of the same merit and of the same..."

---

<page-num>121</page-num>
<header>CHAPTER XV</header>
<sig>R</sig>
<meta>The catchword in the OCR "res, ut" does not match the text flow, which continues from "eiusdem" (of the same) to "sacerdotij" (priesthood).</meta>

"...priesthood. It is the power of wealth or the humility of poverty that makes a Bishop seem either more sublime or lower." And he continues to the point: "Furthermore," he says, "all are successors of the Apostles." Behold! Not only the Pope and the cardinals are successors of the Apostles.

The same is clear through Bede <note>The Venerable Bede (d. 735), an English monk and historian.</note>, who, commenting on that word in Luke 10, "the Lord designated seventy-two others," says: "Just as no one doubts that the twelve apostles prefigured the form of bishops, so these seventy-two are noted to have carried the figure of priests <note>original: "presbyterorum"</note> and the second order of clergy." Behold, from what has been said, it is shown that others besides the Pope and Cardinals can be identified as true successors of the Apostles. Since, therefore, by the ordination of Christ, two orders of clergy sufficed for his church in the time of the Apostles—namely, the Deacon and the Priest—as the saints and the Decree (Distinction 93, chapter "Dominus noster Iesus Christus") say. There it says that the Lord chose the Apostles as Bishops and priests; the Apostles appointed Deacons for themselves after the Lord’s ascension as ministers of their episcopacy and the church. What wonder would it be if almighty God—putting to death this Pope and these Cardinals, and giving them also eternal life if they deserve it—should allow His church to serve <note>original: "militare" - to serve as a soldier of Christ, referring to the "Church Militant" on earth.</note> throughout the whole world in the same order as before, without those 'hinges' <note>Hus is playing on the etymology of "Cardinal," which comes from the Latin 'cardo' meaning 'hinge'. He suggests the church doesn't need these specific human "hinges" to function.</note>? And if He arranged for it to be ruled according to His own unfailing law, giving Bishops and priests who, through preaching the gospel, prayer, and the example of a holy life, would diligently feed the sheep of Christ? For this was the office limited to Peter by Christ, as is clear in the last chapter of John.

<margin>Paul joins the office of pastor and teacher.</margin>
Hence Augustine, in letter 142 to Paulinus, notes how in the Apostle’s text in Ephesians 4, the office of pastor and teacher are joined. "Pastors," he says, "and teachers whom you especially wished to distinguish—I think they are the same, and not that some are pastors and others teachers; and therefore when he had previously said 'pastors,' he added 'teach-'" <note>The text cuts off here, continuing the word "teachers" on the next page.</note>

<summary>Jan Hus argues that the Pope and Cardinals are not the exclusive successors of the Apostles, citing the Council of Pisa and the writings of Jerome and Bede to prove that the early Church functioned with only two orders of clergy: priests and deacons.</summary>
<keywords>Council of Pisa, successors of the apostles, hierarchy, Jerome, Bede, Augustine, priesthood, bishops, deacons, early church, papacy</keywords>

--- Page 69 ---
<meta>This page continues Jan Hus's critique of the institutional hierarchy. Having argued that the Church can exist without the current structure of the Papacy, he now moves to define how one can distinguish between a true minister of Christ and a follower of the "Antichrist" based on their behavior and adherence to scripture rather than human laws.</meta>

<page-num>122</page-num>
<header>122 J. H. ON THE CHURCH</header>

...matters, so that pastors might understand that teaching belongs to their office. For this reason, he <note>referring to the Apostle Paul in Ephesians 4:11</note> did not say, "some indeed as pastors, and some as teachers," as in the previous way of speaking, but "some as pastors and teachers," as if he embraced one thing under two names—some who are both pastors and teachers. From this, once superfluous things are stripped away, it would be clear who remains a true pastor—whether Pope, cardinal, or Bishop—out of the Lord's treasury; and perhaps many useless men, thieves and robbers, would be found rather than true Vicars of the Lord Jesus Christ.

But an objection is raised against these points: if the Pope and cardinals are not the true and manifest successors of the Apostles, then by the same reasoning neither are others, since no distinction can be made because there can be "ravening wolves in sheep’s clothing," as is clear from 
<margin>The twofold clergy: of Christ and of Antichrist</margin>
Matthew 7. Here it is necessary to consider the twofold sect of the clergy, namely the Clergy of Christ and the clergy of Antichrist. The Clergy of Christ rests in its head, Christ, and in His laws; but the Clergy of Antichrist relies either totally or predominantly on human laws and the laws of Antichrist, yet it masks everything to appear as the clergy of Christ and the church, so that the people may be seduced by the pretense. These two contrary groups must rely on two contrary heads with their respective laws. And the evidence of the facts teaches us the descriptions of the members; for it is certain that the Clergy of the church limps between two sides, because of which it is necessary that the Laity, supported by them in opinion and life, should falter in such disparity.
<margin>The Clergy of Antichrist.</margin>
Moreover, these parts can be distinguished topically by this most of all: that the Clergy of Antichrist insists more attentively on human traditions and on defending privileges that savor of the pride or profit of the world; it wishes to live gloriously and luxuriously, unlike Christ, utterly putting behind it the imitation of the character of the Lord Jesus Christ.
<margin>The Clergy of Christ.</margin>
But the clergy of Christ labors diligently for the laws of Christ and His privileges, through which spiritual good is acquired and shown; and it flees

<column-break/>

<page-num>123</page-num>
<header>CHAPTER XVI 123</header>

pride and the pleasure of the world, seeking to live in conformity with Christ, attending most diligently to the following of the Lord Jesus Christ. Nor is it right for a believer to doubt that this part is the true one, and the former is erroneous. And although a traveler <note>original: "viator," a common theological term for a person on their journey through earthly life toward eternity</note> cannot clearly know a truly appointed pastor without a revelation, nevertheless from works that conform to the law of Christ, he ought to suppose that such a one is a pastor. If, however, he sees him living contrary to Christ, how can he judge otherwise than that he is a vicar of antichrist? For Christ says, "By their fruits you shall know them" <note>Matthew 7:20</note>. And in the twelfth chapter of the same: "He who is not with me is against me" <note>Matthew 12:30</note>, where the Ordinary Gloss <note>The *Glossa Ordinaria*, a standard medieval commentary on the Bible</note> says that "he who is not with me" means "he who does works unlike mine is contrary to me." If, therefore, a prelate is proud, lives luxuriously, follows greed, is impatient, and does not feed the sheep but oppresses and scatters them, how is he not an Antichrist? Hence, men can easily recognize the wicked by works that are foreign and contrary to Christ; but the good are not so easily known, because hypocrisy can lie hidden.

### CHAPTER XVI.

The aforementioned Doctors further propose that certain members of the clergy of Bohemia, holding the Pope and the college of cardinals in little regard, do not wish to consent to this, wanting to have only the Holy Scripture as judge in such matters. They interpret this Holy Scripture according to their own heads and wish it to be so interpreted, not caring for the interpretation of the community of the wise in the church, nor paying attention to the Holy Scripture in Deuteronomy 17: "If you perceive a judgment to be difficult and ambiguous between blood and blood, cause and cause, leprosy and non-leprosy, and you see that the words of judgment vary within your gates, rise up and..." <note>original: "Si difficile & ambiguū iudiciū...". Hus is quoting the Latin Vulgate's instructions for seeking higher judicial authority.</note>

He defends his reliance on Scripture as the ultimate judge against accusations that he ignores traditional Church authority.</summary>

Hus is debating the source of authority in the Church—whether it resides solely in the person of the Pope or in the Word of God as found in the Scriptures.</meta>

<margin>Responses should be given according to divine law</margin>
...law, and "go up to the place which the Lord your God has chosen, and you shall come to the priests of the Levitical race, and to the judge who shall be at that time, and you shall ask of them, and they shall judge for you the truth of the judgment, and you shall do whatever they say, who preside over the place which the Lord has chosen, and they shall teach you according to His law; you shall follow their sentence, nor shall you decline to the right hand or to the left. But he who acts proudly, refusing to obey the command of the priest who ministers at that time to your God, and the decree of the judge, that man shall die, and you shall take away the evil from Israel; and all the people, hearing it, shall fear, so that no one thereafter may swell with pride." <note>This is a quotation from Deuteronomy 17:8-12, used here to argue for the authority of religious leaders, though Hus will soon qualify how that authority must be exercised.</note> Now, it is clear to every faithful person that the Roman Church is the place which the Lord has chosen, where the Lord has placed the headship of the entire Church, in which we find the Priest who presides over the place, who is the Pope, the true and manifest successor of Peter. The Cardinals are indeed the "priests of the Levitical race" <note>original: "sacerdotes Levitici generis"</note> who are joined to the Lord Pope in the execution of the priestly office, to whom one must recur in doubts and difficulties, according to the sentence of God, in Catholic and ecclesiastical matters. Regarding this, Jerome speaks to the same effect in an epistle to the Pope, saying: "This is the faith, most blessed Pope, which we have learned in the Catholic Church and which we have always held. If anything has been set down in it less skillfully or cautiously, we desire it to be corrected by you, who hold the seat and the faith of Peter. But if this our confession is approved by the judgment of your Apostleship, whoever wishes to blame me will prove himself to be unskilled, or malevolent, or even not a Catholic, but a heretic."

I estimate that this explanation, regarding its principles, flowed primarily from the head of Stephen Paletz <note>Stephen Paletz was a chief theological opponent of Hus at the Council of Constance</note>. In it, he strives first to incite the Pope and the Cardinals against the party opposing him, when he says: "But certain members of the Bohemian Clergy, regarding the Pope and the college <note>the College of Cardinals</note> as of little value, refuse to consent to this: namely, that the Pope is the head of the Roman Church and the Cardinals are the body, and that they are the true and manifest Vicars of the apostles." Regarding these things, it has been said. Concerning the low
<meta>catchword: estimation</meta>

<header>C H A P T E R XVI</header>

<margin>The Pope and cardinals ought to be held in low esteem because of greed.</margin>
estimation, however, I say that regarding their greed, vanity, and illicit commands, the Pope and the cardinals ought to be held in low regard. For in this way the Savior held "salt that has lost its flavor" <note>original: "sal infatuatum," literally "foolish salt"</note> in low regard, which is good for nothing except to be trodden underfoot by men, in Matthew chapter 5; and in Luke 14 he adds: "It is useful neither for the soil nor for the dunghill." And He held Judas Iscariot in such low regard that He called him a devil in John 6, and the "son of perdition" in John 17. And He called Peter "Satan" when he was contrary to Him, in Matthew 16.

After this, that Doctor <note>Referring again to Stephen Paletz</note> piles up many lies against our party. The first lie is that we wish to have only Holy Scripture as a judge in such matters. In saying this, he asserts that we would wish to have neither God, nor the Apostles, nor the holy doctors, nor the universal church as a judge. He pulled this lie out of a certain cessation <note>A formal cessation of academic activities or a specific legal pause in the dispute</note> that we made mutually, where it was said that he should show Scripture for his sayings, and from the fact that we were unwilling to consent to those adversaries. Yet the doctor ought to know this: that neither to him, nor to all those adhering to him, do we wish to consent in a matter of faith unless they base themselves on Christ in Scripture or reason. I do not expect a revelation to be made to them so that I might feel differently than what Scripture has taught. The second lie that he attributes to our party is that we interpret Holy Scripture "according to our own heads" <note>original: "secundum capita nostra," meaning subjectively or willfully</note>; that is, as he pretends along with other doctors, that we explain Holy Scripture according to our own erroneous concept or will. In this, he accuses us of presumption of understanding and of heresy, but he does so lyingly, because we do not intend, with God's help, to explain Scripture otherwise than as the Holy Spirit demands, and as the holy doctors—to whom the Holy Spirit gave understanding—explain it. And I would wish that this doctor, with all his colleagues, would show which Scripture we explain badly. Hence he is more suspected of lying when he adds, and inter-

<meta>This page continues Jan Hus’s argument against his former colleagues, Stephen Paletz and Stanislaus of Znaim. # J. H. ON THE CHURCH

## CHAPTER XVI

...they wish to fly, for if he is not a knower of hearts, how does he dare to say that we wish to explain the Scripture otherwise than we ought? But he is vexed by this: that we do not stand by his whim along with his colleague Stanislaus, because he considers himself and his adhering doctors to be the only wise ones in the Church. And they are all the more suspect of lying because they have not dared to assert that we do not care for the interpretation of the holy doctors. Instead, they add a complete lie, saying that we do not even pay attention to the Sacred Scripture in Deuteronomy 17: "If there be a hard [and doubtful matter in judgment etc.]." <note>original: "Si difficile &c." A reference to Deuteronomy 17:8-12, which the doctors used to argue for absolute obedience to legal authorities.</note> For this, these doctors ought to know that we *do* pay attention to that Sacred Scripture, and we grant it to be the true Word of God, which actually confirms our own position. For as the diligent expositor of Scripture, Nicholas of Lyra, says regarding the cited text: **"The opinion of no man, of whatever authority he may be, is to be held if it manifestly contains falsehood or error; and this is clear from what is promised in the text: 'They shall judge for you the truth of the judgment.' Afterward it adds: 'And they shall teach you according to His law.'"** <note>Nicholas of Lyra (c. 1270–1349) was the most respected Bible commentator of the Middle Ages; Hus frequently quotes him because his literal interpretations often supported reformist views.</note> From this it is clear that if they speak falsely or manifestly decline from the law of God, they are not to be heard. Thus says Lyra. To confirm this, there is that word of the Lord in Exodus 23: **"You shall not follow the crowd to do evil, nor shall you yield in judgment to the opinion of the many, so as to stray from the truth."** Upon which Lyra himself says that in the Hebrew it has: **"You shall not turn aside after the *Rabim*"** <note>original: "post Rabim"</note>—that is, after the Masters or the great ones—to sin. And further down: **"Because just as one must not turn from the truth because of the majority of those judging who are turning away from it, so one must not turn because of those who are of greater authority in judging."** Thus says Lyra.

<margin>No one ought to obey a prelate against the Scripture.</margin>
Behold, I trust this expositor regarding this opinion more than all the aforementioned Doctors. For he pertinently draws out from the Scripture, first: that the opinion of no man, of whatever authority he may be—and thus not even the Pope’s—is to be held if it manifestly contains falsehood or error. For certain it seems to me that Paletz and Stanislaus, out of fear

<meta>catchword: of the Pope</meta>

<column-break/>

of the Pope and the cardinals, would not dare to publicly confess this holy saying. Second, he draws out that the law of God is the measure according to which, and not otherwise, every judge, and especially ecclesiastical judges, ought to judge. For that law shows what truth ought to be accepted; whence he says, "which is clear through this: 'They shall judge for you the truth of the judgment,' and afterward follows, 'and they shall teach you according to His law.'" Oh doctors, why do you not hold to this Scripture? It was asked of you, and requested for God's sake publicly in the convocation of the university, that you might judge the truth according to the law of God: whether the Bull for the raising of the cross <term>Bull of the Crusade: A decree issued by Pope John XXIII in 1411 calling for a crusade against King Ladislaus of Naples, promising indulgences to those who funded it.</term> obliges the scholars of the university to give subsidies from the goods granted by God to the Pope against Ladislaus and against his accomplices under command. And you responded that you did not wish to inform them, nor to judge or interpret the Pope's Bulls. But in corners you wrote otherwise; and especially I heard Paletz saying of the articles which were presented to him by the Pope’s legates, that they contained manifest errors, palpable to the hand—yet those very articles were extracted from the Bull and presented by those same legates, as the primary Commissioners by the authority of the Pope himself, to be promulgated by the preachers. Wherefore, as I heard, the preacher Master Briccius had said in their college to the Masters that he would rather die than pronounce them; but when Paletz and others retreated, Briccius also retreated, for the letters of the Lord King <note>King Wenceslaus IV of Bohemia, who initially supported the Pope's sale of indulgences before turning against the clergy.</note> which the Legates had in support had terrified them.

<margin>The law of God is the measure of judges.</margin>
<margin>Those speaking falsely are not to be heard.</margin>
Thirdly, Lyra draws from the aforementioned Scripture of the Lord: that if judges speak falsely, or manifestly decline from the law of God, they are not to be heard; because God says, as I have cited: "You shall not follow the crowd to do evil, nor shall you yield in judgment to the opinion of the many, so as to stray from the truth." How then, against this most holy mandate of God, were we supposed to follow the crowd which the assembled Doctors led to the Town Hall, so that those whom they cannot overcome by Scripture or reason,

<meta>catchword: they might oppress</meta>

...and would overwhelm them with terror. The high priests, scribes, and Pharisees did not enter the <term>praetorium</term> <note>The headquarters or judgment hall of a Roman governor.</note> when accusing Jesus, so that they would not be defiled. But these men <note>Hus is referring to his contemporary opponents, Stephen Paletz and Stanislaus of Znaim.</note>, after joyfully gathering the scribes, Pharisees, and elders of the people, entered in; and one of them, who is called Paletz, read in the hearing of everyone: "If there be a hard [matter etc.]" <note>A reference to Deuteronomy 17:8-12 regarding difficult legal cases.</note> and further down: "But he who acts proudly, refusing to obey the decree of the judge, that man shall die." This man <note>Paletz</note> did not fear falling into <term>irregularity</term> <note>A canonical term for a violation that prevents a priest from performing his duties, such as involvement in a death sentence.</note>; if he had been with the Jews in the accusation of Christ, perhaps he would have said: "His blood be upon us and upon our children, because we have a law, and according to that law he ought to die." Therefore, Pilate is not excused just because he heard the high priest along with the magistrates, scribes, and elders of the people, because God says: "You shall not follow a crowd to do evil" etc. <note>original: "Nō sequeris tur. ad fac. ma." from Exodus 23:2.</note> regarding which Lyra says of the Hebrew text: "You shall not turn aside after the *Rabim*" <note>original: "Nō declinabis post Rabim."</note>—that is, after the masters or the great men—to commit sin. Such were the "great men" and "masters" in the praetorium, condemning and decreeing many things which, up to this day, they have not shown were to be condemned, other than by their own whim. And according to the intent of the Doctors in the cited Scripture, some could act like Jews and say that by the command of the high priest Caiaphas or Annas (who then presided in the holy place which the Lord chose) and by the decree of the judge Pilate, Jesus Christ was justly condemned. This contradicts the Book of Acts, chapter 10 <note>This citation likely refers to Paul’s speech in Acts 13:27-28.</note>, where the Apostle Paul says: "Those who dwelt in Jerusalem and their rulers, being ignorant of him and the voices of the prophets which are read every Sabbath, fulfilled them by judging him; and finding no cause of death in him, they asked Pilate that they might kill him." It is clear that in the condemnation of Christ, the high priest was present, the priests of the Levite lineage were present in the place the Lord chose, and the judge Pilate was present; Jesus Christ did not wish to obey them in their evil, although he obeyed God the Father and Pilate by humbly undergoing death.

Was it therefore just that the high priest, with the priests of the Levite lineage, with the magistrates, and the elders of the people, with

<meta>catchword: Pilate</meta>

---

# CHAPTER XVI 129

Pilate and the soldiers, and even with the crowd shouting "Crucify, crucify him!", condemned Christ the Lord? In truth, because it was not charity but hatred, not truth but lies that drove them, and because ignorance of the law of God seduced them, they therefore erred gravely.

<margin>The Pope can err.</margin>
And a similar seduction can happen to the Pope along with the Cardinals, even with our doctors joined to them, so that they might condemn some truth. For if the Apostles chosen by Christ, who had received the Holy Spirit, fell into <term>heresy</term> <note>Hus is likely referring to the Apostles' initial doubt or misunderstanding of the mission of the Church before the full revelation of the Spirit, as discussed by St. Augustine and the Venerable Bede.</note> according to the blessed Augustine and Bede, how have the Pope and Cardinals received greater "first fruits of the Spirit" so that they cannot wander in such a way or even more so? There is no doubt that the Pope and Cardinals must be obeyed as long as they teach the truth according to the law of God, as the authority says: "They shall judge for you the judgment of truth, and you shall do whatever they say and teach you according to His law." But if the *Rabi* <note>The Hebrew word for "Masters."</note>—that is, the masters or the great men, as Lyra says—be they Popes or Cardinals, should command or teach anything apart from the truth, even with the entire Roman <term>Curia</term> <note>The administrative apparatus and court of the Holy See.</note>, the faithful person must not obey when he recognizes the truth. For God says: "You shall not follow a crowd to do evil" etc.

<margin>Daniel, Nicodemus, and the thief obeyed the Scripture.</margin>
Daniel, Nicodemus, and the thief hanging on the cross put this into practice; they did not wish to consent to the crowds in the condemnation of the truth, as the Scripture speaks. For Daniel, as a youth, liberated Susanna by judging the elders and priests from whom wickedness had proceeded (Daniel 13) <note>This refers to the story of Susanna and the Elders, found in the Greek additions to the Book of Daniel.</note>. Nicodemus acted likewise in the council of the high priests and Pharisees when they sent officers to bring Jesus, wishing to hand him over to death. When they said to the officers: "Are you also seduced? Has any of the rulers or the Pharisees believed in him? But this crowd that does not know the law is cursed," Nicodemus said to them: "Does our law judge a man unless it first hears from him..."

<summary>Hus argues that religious leaders, including Popes and Cardinals, are fallible and should not be obeyed if their decrees contradict God's law, citing the unjust condemnation of Christ as the primary example. He highlights historical and biblical figures like Daniel and Nicodemus who resisted corrupt authorities to uphold the truth.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Jan Hus's critique of the legalistic arguments used by his opponents, specifically Stephen Paletz. He is arguing that the law of God, not the whim of high priests or popes, is the true judge of human actions. He uses the example of Nicodemus and the trial of Christ to demonstrate that religious authorities can be wrong while the Scriptures remain the infallible standard.</meta>

# J. H. ON THE CHURCH

<margin>The Law is the judge</margin>
...before it does so? O blessed Nicodemus, you gave such great power to the law of God; you bore witness to the law, showing that it is the judge of man. Behold, our doctors <note>Hus uses "doctors" to refer to the university-trained theologians and high-ranking clergy who opposed his teachings.</note> in their own sayings judge it to be an "inconvenience" that our side wants to have the law as a judge—a judge that judges most justly, who judges no differently than God, the most just judge. You say: "Does our law judge a man, unless it has first heard from him before he acts?" <note>original: "Nunquid lex nostra iudicat hominem, nisi audierit ab ipso priusquam faciat," quoting John 7:51.</note> as if to say, it is not a law unless it judges justly.

To that judge, Christ sent back the high priests, the Pharisees, the scribes, and the Jews who were charging Christ with sin because he did not keep the Sabbath and called God his father. He said: "Search the scriptures; they are the ones that bear witness of me." <note>original: "Scrutamini scripturas, illæ sunt quæ testimonium perhibent de me," from John 5:39.</note> Did Christ not want the scripture to judge the Jews who did not believe in Christ? Certainly he did. Why then do the doctors want the scripture *not* to be the judge? They want themselves to be believed; whatever they themselves condemn, let that be condemned, and whatever they approve, let that be approved. Indeed, this is what they sought and petitioned for in the <term>praetorium</term> <note>The Roman governor's judgment hall.</note>; for this reason they sought signatures from the masters who would speak against the truth. But the counsel of the Pharisees, scribes, and high priests was scattered because the faithful had spoken against them, refusing to give consent unless they heard proof from the law, which contains every truth worthy of belief. If the high priests, Pharisees, priests, and elders of the people had known this law, they would not have condemned Christ; but they condemned and blasphemed him. More learned than they was the thief, who, hanging on the cross, bore witness to Christ, saying: "This man has done nothing wrong." <note>original: "Hic nihil malī gessit" from Luke 23:41.</note>

And as for the primary intention of the doctors, as I see it—by which they intend to prove that the Pope ought to be the judge of all causes, and whoever does not obey him ought to die a physical death—these doctors should be ashamed of their cruel comparison. This is especially true since Christ Jesus our Lord, the <term>Pontiff</term> <note>A term for a high priest or bishop; here Hus applies it to Christ as the ultimate High Priest.</note> of both testaments, neither wished to judge civilly

<column-break/>

# CHAPTER XVI

nor to condemn the disobedient to a physical death. For regarding the first point, he said in the twelfth chapter of Luke: "O man, who appointed me a judge or a divider over you?" <note>original: "O homo, quid me constituīs iudicē aut diuisorē super uos?" from Luke 12:14.</note>

And regarding the second point, he said to the woman caught in adultery, whom the Pharisees said was guilty of death according to the law, in the eighth chapter of John: "Neither will I condemn you; go, and from now on, sin no more." <note>original: "Nec ego te condemnabo, uade iam amplius nolī peccare." from John 8:11.</note>

But perhaps it would be said by the doctors: "This is not to the point, because the law says, he who acts proudly, refusing to obey the command of the priest [shall die]." Behold, I will give the case in its proper form. For in Matthew 18,
<margin>How one erring according to the Gospel is to be corrected</margin>
Christ says: "If your brother sins against you, go and correct him between you and him alone; if he hears you, you have gained your brother. But if he does not hear you, take one or two others with you, so that by the mouth of two or three witnesses every word may stand. And if he does not hear them, tell the church; but if he does not hear the church, let him be to you as a heathen and a tax collector." <note>original: "sit tibi sicut Ethnicus & publicanus."</note> Behold, to whom did the supreme Lord of the law and Pontiff say this? Certainly to Peter, the future Roman Pontiff after him: that he should piously correct the one who errs by himself, rebuke the disobedient before witnesses, and publicize the one who persists in disobedience to the church (that is, to the multitude). And the one who is stubborn and does not obey the church, he should not kill physically, but should avoid him as a public tax collector and a heathen. What grounds are there, then, for arguing by comparison that in the Old Law the disobedient had to be killed in such a way, and also in the Law of Grace? 

Christ's disciples were deceived by that same clever comparison. For when, following the manner of the prophet Elijah, they wanted to consume the Samaritans (who did not want to receive Christ) with heavenly fire, saying: "Lord, do you want us to tell fire to descend from heaven and consume them?"—the most pious High Priest and best teacher rebuked them. For it follows: "And turning, he rebuked them, saying: 'You do not know of what spirit you are; for the Son of Man did not come to destroy souls, but to save them.'" <note>original: "Nescitīs cuius spiritus estīs: filius hominis nō uenit animas pdere, sed saluare," from Luke 9:55-56.</note> The doctors did not pay attention to this holy Gospel, and therefore they joined together...

<summary>Hus argues that the Law of God is the only true judge and that Christ never authorized the church to use physical execution to punish spiritual disobedience. He contrasts the mercy of the Gospel and Christ’s refusal of civil power with the "cruel" demands of contemporary theologians who advocated for the death penalty for those who disobeyed the Pope.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Jan Hus's argument against the legal and theological justifications used by the high clergy to persecute those they deem heretics. He specifically addresses the "bloody corollary"—the practice of the church handing individuals over to the state to be executed—comparing his accusers to the Pharisees who handed Christ over to Pilate.</meta>

# J. H. ON THE CHURCH

<margin>
Heretics are not
to be immediately
put to death.
</margin>
...their own words this bloody corollary <note>A "corollary" is a proposition that follows from one already proven; Hus calls this one "bloody" because it concludes with a death sentence.</note>, saying: "If any of the clergy should be found in Bohemia to be contrary in the aforementioned matters or any part of them, such a person is to be corrected through ecclesiastical censure; but if he refuses to be corrected, he is to be handed over to secular judgment." <note>original: "seculari iudicio est tradendus." This was the formal legal mechanism where the Church "abandoned" a person to the state authorities, who then carried out the death penalty, usually by burning.</note> In this, they certainly follow the high priests, scribes, and Pharisees who—because Christ would not obey them in all things—handed Him over to secular judgment, saying: "It is not lawful for us to put anyone to death." Are they not truly murderers, even more serious than Pilate? The Savior testifies to this, saying to Pilate: "He who delivered me to you has the greater sin." <note>John 19:11</note> For they are the ones to whom Peter spoke in Acts 3: "You denied the Holy and Righteous One, and asked for a murderer to be granted to you, but you killed the author of life." <note>Acts 3:14-15</note>

Then the doctors <note>Theologians and scholars of the institutional church.</note> add their claim, saying: "It is evident to every faithful person that the Roman Church is the place which the Lord chose, where the Lord placed the headship of the whole church, in which the high priest who presides over the place is the Pope, the true and manifest successor of Peter, and the Cardinals are indeed priests of the Levite lineage." <note>The Levites were the hereditary priestly class in the Old Testament; here, Hus's opponents are trying to give the Cardinals a similar ancient biblical authority.</note>

In this statement, the doctors accumulate many things which they do not prove. For when will they prove that it is "evident to every faithful person" regarding that whole estate of theirs, when there are undoubtedly many faithful who know nothing of Rome, of the Pope, or of the Cardinals—and especially that the Pope is the true successor of Peter and the Cardinals are priests of the Levite lineage? Perhaps these doctors call the Roman Church that "place" of which the Savior prophesied in Matthew 24, saying: "When you see the abomination of desolation standing in the holy place (let the reader understand)."

<margin>
The Roman Church
signifies either a
place or an office.
</margin>
For the doctors either call the Roman Church a place—the Basilica of St. Peter—or the Apostolic office. In those two ways, "place" can be understood there, in which the Lord placed the headship of the entire church, because He willed that the apostles Peter and Paul should primarily suffer there, who are the spiritual princes established (according to the aforementioned things) over all the earth, in whom after

<meta>catchword: Christ</meta>

***

<page-num>133</page-num>
# CHAPTER XVI

Christ, the spiritual headship of the church primarily consists. In this church, it is not this Pope, but Christ who is the High Priest who presides over the place (that is, over the Basilica or the apostolic office) and rules the church, His bride.

But if a life contrary to Christ is seen in the Pope—in pride, in greed, in impatience, in ambition, in the extolling of power, and in the weighing of his own law above the Law of Christ—then it appears that the abomination of desolation of Christ’s virtues is "standing in the holy place" where it ought not to stand, as Christ says in Matthew 13 <note>Actually a reference to Matthew 24:15/Mark 13:14.</note>. Hence, if faithful souls were to see the spiritual state of the church on both sides, and how one master of the house used to preside in it—who would graciously accept them by inviting them, feed them by refreshing them, and defend them by helping them as his own household—but now finds one of an entirely opposite condition presiding in that same house, it is no wonder if many are desolated. It is as if a traveler wanted to stay with a true master of a house of magnificent generosity, piety, manageability, and entirely virtuous conversation, but later finds a monstrous beast who tyrannizes over the guests hungrily, deceitfully, cruelly, arrogantly, and treacherously; the traveler entering the house, seeing such a one sitting in the chair of the pious master, would be amazed, troubled, and desolated in no small measure by the sight. Thus can the abomination of desolation be conceived, according to that passage in Zechariah 11:

> Take again the equipment of a foolish shepherd, for behold, I will raise up a shepherd in the land who will not visit the deserted, nor seek the scattered, nor heal the broken, nor nourish that which stands, but he will eat the flesh of the fat ones and tear off their hoofs. Woe to the shepherd and the idol that deserts the flock!

If therefore there should be in the

is a true successor
of Peter, and when
he is not.
</margin>

<summary>Jan Hus challenges the claim that the Pope and Cardinals are the divinely appointed "headship" of the Church regardless of their behavior. He argues that a Pope who lives in greed and pride is not a successor of Peter but is instead the "abomination of desolation" or a "foolish shepherd" prophesied in the Bible.</summary>

--- Page 75 ---
<meta>This page continues Jan Hus’s critique of the institutional Church’s hierarchy. He is arguing that titles like "Most Holy" or "Successor of Peter" are not automatically true by office, but must be earned through Christ-like behavior. He warns against the dangers of flattery and the deification of the papacy.</meta>

# JOHN HUS ON THE CHURCH

<margin>The Pope is not to be called "most holy."</margin>
...an "idol shepherd" who leaves the flock <note>A reference to Zechariah 11:17.</note>; therefore, such a one is the high priest and the true and manifest successor of Peter. This is as false as saying: "He is a pseudo-christ, therefore he is a true and manifest follower of Christ." For the true Christ says in Matthew 24: "Then if anyone says to you, 'Look, here is the Christ!' or 'There he is!' do not believe it. For false christs and false prophets will arise." Therefore, the faithful must take care lest, out of flattery, they call a pseudo-christ "most holy," or an idol "the high priest" and "the true successor of the apostle Peter." For those who called the "Holy Father" and "High Priest" presiding over the Roman Church "Agnes"—who actually gave birth—were surely deceived. <note>original: "Agnetem quae peperit realiter". This refers to the medieval legend of Pope Joan (often called Agnes), who was said to have disguised herself as a man, been elected Pope, and was discovered when she gave birth during a procession.</note>

Now, regarding the saying of the blessed Jerome, "This is the faith of the most blessed Pope," which is cited in the law <note>Hus refers to the *Decretum Gratiani*, a collection of canon law, specifically "24. q. 1."</note>, it is said that he spoke "reputatively"—meaning by way of courtesy or reputation—based on the apostolic works of Pope Damasus. This is similar to how he wrote to the Blessed Augustine in his letters, saying: "To the truly holy lord and most blessed Pope Augustine." For the saints also speak "reputatively" of prelates, even when they see them turning away from the footsteps of Christ, saying that they are to be condemned as members of the devil. But woe to those who, seeing in a Pope works directly contrary to Christ, still call him "Most Holy Father." For Isaiah 5 says: "Woe to you who call evil good." Such people, through their lying flattery, deceive both themselves and him. For Isaiah 3 says: "O my people, those who call you blessed deceive you, and destroy the way of your paths; the Lord stands up to judge."

<margin>How the Pope and Cardinals are worshiped like gods.</margin>
Indeed, if those learned in the law would boldly tell the truth to the Pope and Cardinals, and not stroke them with flattery out of fear or for the sake of promotion to church benefits, perhaps they might eventually recognize themselves and not allow themselves to be worshiped as gods. But because both sides sin in this—the first side hypocritically rejoicing in the honors and the title of "Most Blessed," and the second side deceitfully tickling them with flattery—both sides must necessarily be cast down. For Isaiah 9 says: "For those who lead this people cause them to err, and those who are led by them are destroyed." <note>original: "praecipitati", literally "cast headlong" or "hurled down".</note>

<column-break/>

# CHAPTER XVI

...destroyed. And who these people are, the prophet shows immediately before, saying: "The Lord will cut off from Israel head and tail, branch and rush, in one day. The elder and honorable man is the head; and the prophet who teaches lies is the tail." Behold, the prophet himself explains what the "head" and the "tail" are. Let anyone who wishes note who is called the "honorable and long-lived father" whom they call the "head." It could probably be said that every pope, from the first to the last, who lives contrary to Christ—whom they have called or will call "head" and "Most Holy Father"—is that "honorable and long-lived one," because such a succession began a long time ago. The "tail," however, which covers up the works of that long-lived father with flattery, or false interpretation, or excuses—and the prophet who teaches lies—is

<margin>Some make the Pope a "mixed god," because he can do all things and is sinless.</margin>
the learned clergy. They teach that the Pope is neither God nor man, but a <term>mixed god</term> <note>original: "deus mixtus". A term used by extreme canonists to suggest the Pope held a status between the human and the divine.</note> or an "earthly god." They teach that the Pope can give me someone else's property and I will be safe; that the Pope can depose a bishop without cause; that he can grant dispensations against the Apostle <note>Referring to the writings/rules of St. Paul.</note>, against an oath, against a vow, or against natural law; and that no one has the right to say to him, "Why are you doing this?" For he can legally say:
-> "So I will, so I command; let my will stand for reason." <-
<note>This is a famous quote from the Roman satirist Juvenal (Satire 6), used here to characterize tyrannical or arbitrary power.</note>
And thus he is "impeccable" <note>Incapable of sinning.</note>, and they say he cannot commit <term>simony</term> <note>The sin of buying or selling sacred things or church offices.</note> because all things are his, and therefore he can do with them as he pleases. They even claim he can command the angels, and save men or damn those he wishes. What is more, he can cause both the papacy and the subject people to bow down, along with those who flatteringly bend their knees before the "head" and the "honorable" one of this world. For by sowing such lies, they lead the Pope, the people, and themselves into a ditch. It is likely about such as these that Christ spoke in Matthew 24: "False christs and false prophets will arise and show great signs and wonders, so as to lead astray, if possible, even the elect."

Returning, however, to the proposition of the Blessed Jerome, it is said that...

<meta>This page continues Jan Hus’s argument regarding the authority of the Pope. He is specifically addressing a quote by St. Jerome often used by supporters of papal supremacy. Hus argues that Jerome's praise for a particular Pope was based on that individual's current reputation for goodness, not a blanket endorsement of the office itself or a guarantee of the Pope's salvation.</meta>

# JOHN HUS ON THE CHURCH

...spoken to that Pope "reputatively" <note>original: "reputative". Hus uses this term to mean "based on reputation" rather than absolute divine truth.</note> on account of his good works. However, whether Jerome had a revelation concerning the predestination <term>Predestination: The theological concept that God has already determined who will be saved.</term> of this Pope and the righteousness of his works remains unknown. 

He says secondly, that the Blessed Jerome speaks this way to the Pope because he was secure in the faith which he wrote; for in that letter, he wrote things expressly contained in Scripture and in the creeds of the church, as can be seen by anyone willing to read the letter. Hence he says: "This is the faith which we learned in the Catholic Church, which we have always held." And it is clear how Jerome’s conditional statement is to be understood: that if his confession of faith is confirmed by the judgment of that Pope, then whoever wished to attack it would be a heretic. For he said and asserted this "reputatively"—not from revelation or the certainty of faith—believing that the Pope would confirm nothing but the truth, nor would he amend what had long been well-held in the church. 

But it would be madness to believe that this must be applied as a consequence to every Roman pontiff, because it is certain that many of them ratified errors and heresies, for they were heretics themselves. Hence, in Distinction 22, the text titled "In the name of the Lord" <note>This refers to the *Decretum Gratiani*, the standard collection of canon law in the Middle Ages.</note> relates how the Pope complains that the See has been frequently struck by the repeated blows of the **Simoniacal heresy** <term>Simony: The sin of buying or selling church offices or spiritual things.</term>. Therefore, wishing to provide a remedy for the future, he decreed that upon the death of a Pope,
<margin>The Electors of the Pope.</margin>
the religious Cardinals, Clergy, and Laity should meet to elect a suitable Pope from the heart of that church or another, wherever the most suitable person might be found. This was always saving the privilege of Emperor Henry, who, along with his heirs, was to have a role in the election of the Pope. Once elected, and even before his consecration—following the example of the Blessed Gregory—he shall have the power to manage the goods of the church. Anyone who hinders this order is anathematized <note>Excommunicated or cursed.</note> as the worst sort of antichrist. 

Regarding this, the **Ordinary Gloss** <note>The standard commentary on the margin of legal and biblical texts.</note> says that here it is secretly hinted what is read in the Chronicles: how Benedict IX, who succeeded Stephen, was elected to the papacy, and John, the Bishop of Sabina, was

was made Pope for money, and was given the name Sylvester. But he was then cast down, and Benedict was restored; then Benedict was ejected again, and the papacy was given to John the Archpriest "before the Latin Gate," who was given the name Gregory. This man was then cast down by Emperor Henry and moved across the mountains. All of this happened within a single year. It was for this reason that this privilege was granted to Henry. Thus says the Gloss of the Decree.

<margin>First</margin>
And as is gathered from the Chronicles of Martin, Chester, and Rudolf: in the year of our Lord 420, Pope Boniface presided in Rome. Against him, Eulalius was ordained, and because of this the church was divided. By the order of Honorius Augustus, both were ordered to leave the city. Thus, after Eulalius was rejected, Boniface, who had been ordained first, was restored to the Apostolic See by the order of Augustus. 

<margin>Second</margin>
Secondly, in the year of our Lord 493, Lawrence was ordained against Pope Symmachus through a struggle for power. 

<margin>III</margin>
Thirdly, in the year of our Lord 768, after the schismatic Pope Constantine was deprived of his eyes, Pope Stephen was appointed. He gathered a synod at Rome and re-ordained those who had been ordained by the schismatic Constantine. 

<margin>IIII.</margin>
Fourthly, in the year of our Lord 873, Anastasius invaded the prelacy against Pope Benedict. 

<margin>V.</margin>
Fifthly, in the year of our Lord 907, Pope Leo presided, against whom Christopher rose up. 

<margin>VI</margin>
Sixthly, in the year of our Lord 968, a synod of bishops from all of Italy was gathered, and Pope John was accused of wicked crimes. When he hesitated to come to excuse himself, Leo—who was still a layman—was appointed Pope by the election of all and the consent of the Emperor. Thus Leo performed ordinations and other acts that were Apostolic. But it was not long before the Romans, having changed their loyalty to the Emperor, received Pope John back. John, having gathered a synod, deposed Leo and voided his acts. It was decreed that the synod held by Leo should not be called a synod, but a **brothel** <note>original: "prostibulum". A term used by contemporaries to describe the extreme corruption and moral decay of the papacy in the 10th century.</note> favoring adulterers. Therefore, whoever had been condemned by Leo's ordination was ordered to sign their own proscription...

<meta>This page continues Jan Hus’s argument regarding the historical corruption and instability of the papacy. He provides a chronological list of papal schisms and conflicts to demonstrate that the office is not inherently holy or stable, but has frequently been subject to human greed, simony, and political interference.</meta>

<margin>VII.</margin>
...present on a paper containing these words: "My father had nothing for himself, and gave nothing to me." And so they remained deposed in those ranks which they had held from *Leo*, not yet ordained. This Pope *John* <note>original: "Ioannes"; likely referring to John XII, infamous for his scandalous lifestyle</note>, while amusing himself with the wife of a certain man, was struck by the devil during the act of intercourse and died without the Lord’s viaticum <term>Viaticum: The final reception of the Eucharist given to a person near death.</term>. Seventh,

<margin>VIII.</margin>
it happened that the Romans, against the oath they had made to the emperors—namely, that they would never elect a Pope without his consent and the consent of his son *Otto*—appointed *Benedict* as Pope. But the emperor, having besieged Rome, afflicted the Romans to such an extent that they promised to receive Pope *Leo*, and thus *Benedict* was restored. Eighth, in the year of our Lord 1047, *Benedict* <note>original: "Benedict⁹", referring to Benedict IX</note>, who had invaded the Papacy through simony <term>Simony: The sin of buying or selling church offices or spiritual privileges.</term>, since he was unlearned in letters, consecrated another Pope along with himself to carry out the duties of the ecclesiastical office,

<margin>IX.</margin>
namely *Sylvester*. Since this did not please many, a third was brought in, *Gregory*, who alone would fulfill the duties of the two. Ninth, in the year of our Lord 1046, while one was arguing against two and two against one over the papacy at *Rome*, King *Henry* went to *Rome* against them. After they were deposed, *Clement* presided, and *Henry* was blessed as emperor by him. The Romans swore that they would never elect a Pope without their consent. Then indeed was the law of the Third Book of Kings, chapter 2 <note>original: "iij. Reg. ij."; in modern Bibles, this is 1 Kings 2, where King Solomon deposes the priest Abiathar</note> put into practice: that like *Solomon*, a king should, in certain cases, depose a High Priest. Then also King *Henry* humbly received a blessing from him, and then afterward the confirmation was made, without which there should be no other emperor. But what is this necessary to the law of God? Here it is held that before the introduction of the Cardinals, the Pope was elected by the Roman people. Tenth, in the year of our Lord 1068, while two were

<margin>X.</margin>
contending for the papacy at *Rome*, *Alexander* was received after he had purged his innocence regarding simony, and *Cadalus*, Bishop of *Parma*, was rejected. Whence it was said: *“Cadalus in Parma was through me both bow and arms; Cadalus fell, and Parma was the ruin.”* <note>original: "Cadalus in Parma per me fuit arcus & arma Cadalus occubuit, Parma ruina fuit." <margin>XIII.</margin>
in the year of our Lord 1091, it is said there were two who were called Roman Pontiffs, disagreeing with one another and dragging the church of God divided between them: *Urban*, who had first been the Bishop of *Ostia*, and *Clement*, who was *Wibert*, the Archbishop of *Ravenna*.

<margin>XIV.</margin>
Fourteenth, in the year of our Lord 1130, while Pope *Innocent* was reigning, *Peter Leonis* <note>Peter son of Leo</note> was intruded and called *Anacletus*; and *Innocent* crossed over into *France*, whence it is said: *“Peter has Rome, but [Innocent] has the whole world of the flock.”* Fifteenth, in the year

<margin>XV.</margin>
of our Lord 1179, Pope *Albert* <note>This likely refers to the conflict surrounding Alexander III</note> reigned. Against him *Octavian* was intruded, but he died in schism; and so did *Guido of Crema*, but *John*, the intruder, was reconciled. Thus, within every hundred-year period from the time of the church's endowment <note>original: "dotationis ecclesiæ"; Hus refers to the "Donation of Constantine," the grant of lands and power to the church which he viewed as the source of its corruption.</note>, a notable contention has occurred between Popes. In our own times, a dispute began between *Urban VI*, who resided at *Rome*, and *Robert of Geneva*, who held his seat in *Avignon*. This dispute lasted among their successors until the year of our Lord 1409, in which year, at the *Council of Pisa*, after two had been condemned as heretics—namely, *Gregory* and *Benedict*—*Alexander* of the *Order of Friars Minor* <note>The Franciscans</note> was elected Pope. After he died, three contenders remained: *Pope John XXIII*, *Gregory* in *Sicily*, and *Benedict* in *Spain*. From whence this diabolical contention originally came, even a blind man can feel out the moving cause:

<margin>The possession of the church is its decrease in virtues</margin>
because it comes from the endowment. Hence the Blessed *Jerome* wrote in the *Lives of the Fathers*: *"From the time the church grew in possessions, it decreased in virtues."* And the probability of the Chronicle which the *Chesterite* <note>Ranulf Higden, a monk of Chester and author of a popular world history called the *Polychronicon*</note> narrates in book four, chapter 86, is clear: how at the

<margin>Note.</margin>
time of the church's endowment, an angelic voice was heard in the air, saying: *"Today poison has been poured into the holy church of God."* For how...

<summary>Jan Hus details a history of papal schisms and corruption, arguing that the Church has declined in virtue ever since it acquired worldly wealth and political power (the "Donation of Constantine"). He cites various historical conflicts, including the Great Western Schism of his own era, as proof of this spiritual decay.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Jan Hus's argument about the nature of the Papacy. He concludes a section arguing that the truth can be spoken even by wicked men or demons, and that the office of the Pope does not automatically make the occupant holy or infallible.</meta>

# ON THE CHURCH

however it may be, this is true: whether a good angel gave the voice, or the devil, because it is certain that demons, who rejoice when they have done evil, must serve God and be messengers of truth; and it is fitting for God to proclaim danger to the people through the "mammon of iniquity" <note>original: "mammonā iniquitatis"; a reference to Luke 16:9, here suggesting God uses even corrupt authorities to deliver warnings.</note> as His herald. From these things, a faithful person can understand whether, by the mere fact that someone is named Pope, he is the supreme pontiff of the church, the most blessed father, the most learned in the faith above all followers of Christ, and the head of God’s holy church.

->With what intention John Hus resisted the Roman prelates. Also, obedience is understood and defined in many ways according to whether it pertains to commands and counsels.<-

### CHAPTER XVII

Further, regarding the main point, by which the aforementioned doctors believe all their sayings to be necessary or true, they posit: That the Apostolic See of the Roman Church and its Prelates must be obeyed by those of lower rank in all things whatsoever, where "pure good" <note>Actions that are always good, like loving God.</note> is not prohibited, or "pure evil" <note>Actions that are always sinful, like murder.</note> is not commanded, but rather the "middle" ground <term>Middle things: Also known as 'adiaphora,' these are actions that are neither commanded nor forbidden by God, such as specific customs or clothing.</term>, which according to the manner, place, time, or person can be both good and evil, according to the sentence of the Savior in Matthew 23: *All things whatsoever they tell you, observe and do.* And they add, according to *Bernard* <note>St. Bernard of Clairvaux, a highly influential 12th-century mystic and monk.</note> in his letter to the monk *Adam* which begins, "If you remain in charity," etc.: The law of obedience is placed as if on the tree of the knowledge of good and evil, which was in the middle of Paradise. In these matters, it is certainly not right to prescribe our own sense over the judgment of the Masters; in these things, neither the command nor the prohibition of the Prelates is to be despised. And they add: "But certain members of the Clergy in the kingdom of Bohemia refuse to consent, striving as much as they can to lead the faithful people into disobedience regarding the prelates, and to irreverence toward the papal, episcopal, priestly, and..."

<meta>catchword: clerical, and</meta>

***

<page-num>141</page-num>
<header>CHAPTER XVII</header>

clerical, and clerical dignities, not considering that saying of the blessed *Augustine* in his 86th sermon, speaking in these words: "If you fast by days and perform prayer by nights, if you are in sackcloth or in ashes, if you do nothing else except what is commanded to you in the law, and you are wise in your own eyes, and you were not obedient to your father (understand not the physical father, but the spiritual one), you have lost all virtues." From this, they argue that obedience is worth more than all other moral virtues. Thus he says.

<margin>Commendation of obedience.</margin>
In this combination of arguments, the doctors mix truths with falsehoods, flattery, and terror; these are all entwined in these words: *But certain members of the Clergy*, noting our side there, *refuse to consent, striving as much as they can to lead the faithful people into disobedience.* Behold a false lie, by which they imply that we are seducers of the people, since it is not the intention of our side to seduce the people from true obedience, but rather that the people may be one, ruled from the heart by the law of Christ.

<margin>The most just intention of Jan Hus and those adhering to him.</margin>
Second, the intention of our side is that Antichristian <note>Hus frequently used "Antichristian" to describe laws or practices he believed were contrary to the Gospel.</note> constitutions should not infatuate the people or divide them from Christ, but that the law of Christ should reign sincerely alongside the customs of the people approved by the law of the Lord. And third, the intention of our side is that the Clergy live sincerely according to the gospel of Jesus Christ, with pomp, greed, and luxury left behind. And fourth, our side hopes and preaches that the Church Militant <term>Church Militant: The Christian church on earth, struggling against sin and the devil.</term>, arranged sincerely according to the parts which the Lord ordained, should be composed of: priests of Christ purely keeping His law; the nobles of the world compelling people to the observance of Christ’s ordinance; and the common people serving both of these parts according to the law of Christ. Therefore, let the doctors grant this injury to our side. Moreover, the flattery which they show to the Prelates, and the terror with which they frighten our side, are involved in these words: *striving to lead the faithful people into disobedience regarding the Prelates, and to irreverence toward the papal, episcopal, priestly, and clerical dignities.* Blessed be Christ.

He outlines his vision for a reformed church where the clergy, nobility, and commoners all live according to the Gospel rather than human traditions.</summary>
